# Test review: report

**Status:** Open — every test has a verdict; the plan's jobs are next

Every test in the repository has a verdict from `skills/testing/REVIEWING.md` of SushiSkills, reviewed
on 2026-11-05 as part of J15 (`docs/agent/2026_11_03_INDUSTRIAL_TESTING/` in SushiSkills). The review read
the code and ran nothing. Each record names the files it read. The tests of the shared checkers under
`tools/` are reviewed once, in SushiSkills; this report covers this repository's own tests and its own
checkers' tests.

## What the review found

- **Bare tolerances and loops** drive most rewrites: thresholds, dB bounds and float tolerances written
  as literals, with assertions inside loops over blocks, bands or samples. 25 cases use
  `EXPECT_NEAR(..., 0.0)`, an exact comparison spelled as a near one. No `tests/support/tolerance.hpp`.
- **`node_registry()`.** The rig, session and topology tests reach it through
  `descriptor_is_registered` and `seed_defaults` in `slot_graph.cpp` (71 resizes). If the registry is an
  immutable catalogue once seeded, those tests stay as they are; if not, a registry passed in is the
  seam. `test_rig_controller.cpp` keeps none of its 102 tests.
- **Test-written stand-ins** for `AudioNode`, `AudioDevice`, `CabinetHost`, the DK statistics and the DK
  glue (`LinearPort<N>`, `PoisonablePort`, `NoNonlinearity`), with no in-memory adapter beside them.
- **Process-wide state:** `node_counters`, `RigGraph::counters`, `RoomNode::counters()`,
  `Counted::live_count`, `LogCapture`, the profiler's session and `Collector` name table, the
  `AllocationTrap` hook on `operator new`, and `SUSHIDSP_PLUGIN_RIG`, which `ClapRigPlugin::init` reads.
- **Long small tests:** dd3 acceptance tests labelled `Small_` render 0.6 s to 5 s per case; two power-amp
  cases run about 7.68 M samples; the DK convergence test runs about a million solves; two `Small_` DK
  tests read LTspice CSVs.
- The CLI tests stub their own functions or use the `project` fixture (85 of 114); they wait on
  sushicore's in-memory runner and console.

## Defects the review found

- `compare_or_regenerate` returns -200 under `SUSHIDSP_REGENERATE_GOLDEN`, so a golden passes after a
  silent rewrite. A regenerate run must not report a pass.
- The jcm800 and peavey5150 alias-floor tests call `GTEST_SKIP` before their only assertion: nothing runs.
- Tests that cannot fail: `RoomNodeMicrophoneListenerFillsBothChannelsIdentically` (`render_both` copies
  one channel to both, `test_behaviour.cpp` lines 391 to 392); `FirFilter TapCountIsTheHrtfTapCount`
  compares `K_TAPS` with itself; `HeadHolderKeepsTheSourceItWasGiven` sets what it reads;
  `Jcm800IsDcStableAcrossManyBlocksAtHighPreamp` checks finiteness only;
  `DampedNewtonWithHalvingDisabledFailsOnTheDivergentStart` checks only `isfinite`, though the dk README
  says it pins the stall guard.
- `WavWriterFloat32HeaderCarriesAnExtensibleFmtChunkAndAFactChunk`: `wav_writer.cpp` writes an 18-byte
  WAVEFORMATEX with tag 3, not EXTENSIBLE.
- Names that disagree with their bodies: `LongTailedPair ResetClearsBothHalves`, `GraphicEqualizer
  CutsOneBandAndClampsSliders`, `PerBandQNarrowsOneBandOnly`, `PowerSupplySag
  RecoversTowardUnityAfterSilence`, `SoftplusIsTheIdentityAboveThirty`; DenseLu cases sit under a
  `DampedNewtonSolver` suite name.
- Comments and assertions that disagree: `test_dd3_analog_filters.cpp` says 0.4 % and allows 1.5 %; the
  LateReverb bound is 0.05 where the comment derives 0.0065.
- Missing tests: the `*PerformsNoHeapAllocation` tests of `FmvToneStack`, `GraphicEqualizer` and
  `ResonanceControl`; `take_newton_tally()` of `TriodeStage` and `PentodeStage`; `sushidsp.jcm800` in
  the registry list test. `sushidsp_profile_names` omits 24 zone names of five node regions.
- `benchmark_triode_stage.cpp` asserts nothing. `tests/budget_exemptions.toml` is absent.
- Thresholds of -110, -40 and -20 dB have no derivation in the source; expected values in the room tests
  restate formulas (Eyring, diffuse field, the late reverb share).
- `tests/foundation/math/dk/test_dk_circuit/README.md` cites "Tasks 6 and 7"; the namespace is
  `SushiDSP::Room` in one place and `SushiDSP::room` in another.
- `tools/testing/check_test_additions.py` and its test are a leftover: SushiSkills removed the checker in
  phase B, and the copier never removes a file upstream dropped. Both are deleted.
- `tools/tests/test_test_budget_settings.py` skips here, since the repository has no `skills/` folder, so
  its promise is checked nowhere; it also defines `read_budget` and `differences` in the test file.

## Decisions

The owner delegated these decisions on 2026-11-05. Each ruling below is final for the plan; its reason follows it.

1. `node_registry()` is an immutable catalogue once seeded. Seeding finishes before any test or caller reads it, and no public call changes it afterwards. A reader of a constant catalogue is small, so the 71 resizes become keeps. If the job finds a call that changes the catalogue after seeding, that call is removed.
2. The allocation trap is medium. It replaces the process-wide `operator new`, so it runs in `Medium_` suites, scoped by an RAII guard, in the unit's own test file as the repository's `CLAUDE.md` asks. The testing skill names it as a medium boundary.
3. The WAV writer test is renamed to what the code writes, an 18-byte `WAVEFORMATEX` with tag 3 and a fact chunk, which is valid for one or two float channels. EXTENSIBLE is written only for more than two channels, and that case gets its own test if the writer accepts it.
4. The -110, -40 and -20 dB thresholds are characterized, each with a backlog entry, until a derivation is written beside it.
5. A named constant holding a hand-typed epsilon is not a typed tolerance. A tolerance names its kind (absolute, relative or ulps) and its derivation, so every such constant is redefined through `tolerance.hpp`, keeping its name. The rewrites stay rewrites, and they are mechanical.
6. A regeneration under `SUSHIDSP_REGENERATE_GOLDEN` returns the 0 dB residual a missing file returns, and logs a `WARN`, so every golden test of that run fails and a second run confirms the pins. `compare_or_regenerate` gains an overload that takes a `GoldenMode`, and `golden_mode_from_environment()` is the one reader of the variable, so a test can name the mode without the environment (T2, 2026-11-09).
7. The in-memory adapters are public headers of the module that owns each port: `InMemoryNode`, `InMemoryAudioDevice` and `InMemoryCabinetHost` in `core`, `ConductancePort<N>` and `PoisonablePort` in `math/dk`. `InMemoryNode` is one configurable type rather than one per stand-in, since every stand-in differed only in shape and level; an unset shape field answers with `AudioNode`'s default, so the default tests can use it. The builders the graph tests share are in `tests/support/in_memory_nodes.hpp`. `tube_screamer_circuits::LinearPort` becomes an alias of `dk::LinearPort`, the one copy (T3, 2026-11-09).
8. Decision 1 holds as found: `node_registry()` returns a `static const` vector built on first call, and no public call changes it, so no call is removed. The process-wide counters get seams instead of a rewrite of their owners: `RigGraph(block_size, buffers, Counters&)`, `RoomNode(Counters&)`, the `NodeCounters&` overloads of `note_output_clamp`, `note_newton_solves` and `flush_dk_statistics`, and the `CoreCounterReport` constructor that takes both sets. A host keeps the process-wide sets; each small test passes its own. Decision 2 is applied by moving the 41 cases that arm an `AllocationTrap` from `Small_` suites into `Medium_` suites of the same name. `ClapRigPlugin` takes its startup rig text in its constructor; `create()` reads `SUSHIDSP_PLUGIN_RIG` through `read_startup_rig()` and passes it, so the read moves from `init()` to `create()` and the load stays in `init()` (T4, 2026-11-09).
9. T6 takes the defects in the order listed above (2026-11-09). Fixed: the regeneration pass (T2); the WAV writer test renamed to `WavWriterFloat32HeaderCarriesAnEighteenByteFloatFmtChunkAndAFactChunk`, since the writer never writes EXTENSIBLE (decision 3); `FirFilter TapCountIsTheHrtfTapCount` deleted, a constant compared with the constant it is defined as; `HeadHolderKeepsTheSourceItWasGiven` replaced by two tests of `refresh_head`; the microphone test replaced by `RoomNodeMicrophoneIgnoresAListenerMovedMidRender`, which compares two renders, since the microphone port is mono and the old test copied it to both channels; `Jcm800IsDcStableAcrossManyBlocksAtHighPreamp` renamed to what it checks, `Jcm800StaysFiniteAcrossManyBlocksAtHighPreamp`; the five names that disagreed with their bodies renamed, the graphic equalizer one split in two; the eight DenseLu cases moved to `test_dense_lu.cpp` under `Small_DenseLu`; the three missing `*PerformsNoHeapAllocation` tests and two `take_newton_tally()` tests per stage added; `sushidsp.jcm800` added to the registry's amplifier test; the 24 region zone names added to the profile names test; the stale "Tasks 6 and 7" citation replaced; `tests/budget_exemptions.toml` added with no entry. Not defects: `benchmark_triode_stage.cpp` is a benchmark, which ends with a measurement and no verdict; `DampedNewtonWithHalvingDisabledFailsOnTheDivergentStart` asserts `EXPECT_FALSE(solve)`, which fails when the stall guard is gone, so it pins the guard as its README says. Covered by an open entry: the two alias-floor skips (RW-4). Left for the orchestrator to enter in the backlog, since a worker writes none: the -110, -40 and -20 dB thresholds (decision 4, one entry each); the dd3 anti-alias bound (comment 0.4 %, assertion 1.5 %) and the LateReverb click bound (comment 0.0065, assertion 0.05), which need a derived or measured bound before either side changes; the `SushiDSP::Room` and `SushiDSP::room` namespaces of `nodes/room`, a rename across 13 headers and sources and their tests; `tools/tests/test_test_budget_settings.py`, a copied shared test whose fix belongs in SushiSkills. Risks for the final build: the new tally tests assume a -50 V to +50 V ramp takes each stage off its bounds at least once, and the new trap tests assume `set_band`, `set_depth` and the tone setters are the only allocating calls.
10. The M6 seams, ruled by the owner on 2026-11-10; the default behaviour of each is unchanged. `measure_unrecorded` takes a clock argument: the port is any type with `double now_nanoseconds()`, passed as a template argument so the timed repetition gains no indirect call; `SteadyNanosecondClock` (`nanosecond_clock.hpp`) is the production adapter and the old four-argument call, and `StepNanosecondClock` (`in_memory_nanosecond_clock.hpp`) is the in-memory adapter, so the unrecorded-path tests are `Small_`. `run_selected(cases, argc, argv)` takes a case list and the old call passes `registered_cases()`; `registered_cases()` itself keeps no parameter, since a getter of the process-wide list has nothing to take, so the second half of the ruling is a `BenchmarkRegistration` constructor that adds to a list it is given. `measure_node(node, name, configure)` and `profile_node(node, warmup, active)` take a node instance; the template-only calls construct a `Node` and delegate, so the tests use `InMemoryNode`. `dk::InMemoryDkCounters` (`in_memory_dk_counters.hpp`) answers the four statistics of `DkCircuit`. One coverage change: `ProfileNodeProcessesOneBlockPerPeriodAndRecordsItsZone` checked a zone the test node wrote itself, which `InMemoryNode` does not; it becomes two tests that check the blocks processed and the warm-up, active and timeline periods the run records, which is `profile_node`'s own promise (M6, 2026-11-10).

100. M8, lane A (2026-11-10). `tests/foundation/circuits/stages/benchmark_triode_stage.cpp` is kept, not deleted. The record says delete and move any claim to the stage's test, but the file asserts no claim, and it is a registered case of the `sushidsp_bench` program that `sd bench` runs (`tests/CMakeLists.txt`, `add_executable(sushidsp_bench ...)`); the benchmark registry still registers it, and `docs/agent/2026_10_26_PROFILER_AND_LOG_ADOPTION/SPEC.md` cites it. The ruling deletes only when nothing else registers the file, so it stays; the test runner never ran it.

101. M8, lane A (2026-11-10). The model tests (`test_pentode_el34.cpp`, `test_triode12_ax7.cpp`) hold the Koren oracle of `models/README.md`, printed to 15 digits, to half a unit of the last printed digit plus 64 u of the model's own rounding (the longest chain is the softplus argument, the softplus, a power, an arctangent and the products). Jacobian entries are held against a centred difference whose truncation is estimated as three times the gap between the differences over the step and over twice the step. Each loop grid became a row table; a row table of 12 to 16 representative points replaced the 60 to 90 point Cartesian grids of the joint-form and finiteness tests, keeping every extreme value of each axis. The EL34 and 12AX7 tests share no helper header: the two oracle helpers are short and each file stays one brick.

102. M8, lane A (2026-11-10), the log seam ruled by the owner. `foundation/log_capture` already holds the in-memory sink, so no seam is built: `FmvToneStack` and `PowerAmpFeedback` log tests use `LogCapture`, which swaps the process-wide sink for the test's life and restores stderr, and are `Small_` under the testing skill's exception for a scoped in-memory sink. The throw and the log line are two tests each. Default behaviour is unchanged.

103. M8, lane A (2026-11-10). The alias-floor tests of the triode and pentode stages share `tests/support/oversampled_stage_node.hpp`, a template over the stage type that removes the two copies of the 8x-region `AudioNode` wrapper; it is a wrapper that drives the real stage, not a stand-in for a repository-owned type. The tone-stack and feedback-leg tests share no helper yet: both carry a copy of `steady_gain_db` and of the bilinear warp bound (`x^2 / 3` of the slope, `x = pi f / fs`, factor 1.5), and a shared `tests/support` header is the follow-up.

104. M8, lane A (2026-11-10). Two findings from the pentode `test_behaviour.cpp` rewrite. The pair test `PentodePairIsOddSymmetricInsideTheWindow...` was vacuous: its even part is `(c(u) + c(-u)) / 2` with `c(u) = a(u) - b(-u)`, which is exactly zero for any two stages with equal bias, so the equal-bias half became `PentodeStagesOfEqualBiasGiveEqualOutputs` (no shared state) and the bias-difference half compares the even part with a double reference. And `TriodeStageGridConductsOnTheNormanLaw` read the peak excursion, which for a positive input is the largest Newton iterate; the rewrite bounds the overshoot with the quadratic Newton constant `C = max f'' / (2 min f')`, four iterations leaving `(C e0)^16 / C`, and a bound that is void (`C e0 >= 1`) is NaN so the check fails.

105. M9, lane A (2026-11-10). `tests/foundation/core/test_rig_graph/test_channels_adapt.cpp` has no record because it is new: it is the `RigGraph::channels_adapt` row table that `Small_ChannelSelection.RigOutputAdaptsToDeviceCoversTheGraphsTable` was rewritten to stop duplicating. It is `Small_`, has no tolerance and no control flow in a body, and stays. Style finding: its namespace body is not indented like the other test files; formatting only, not changed.

106. M9, lane A (2026-11-10), the owner's ruling on `IgnoresAParameterWrittenByIndexByDefault`. `InMemoryMinimalNode` (`in_memory_minimal_node.hpp`, beside `InMemoryNode`) overrides only the pure virtual methods of `AudioNode`, so `AudioNode::set_parameter_for_audio_thread` is reachable through it. The test stays in `test_audio_node.cpp` and runs on it, and the adapter has two contract tests of its own (`test_in_memory_minimal_node.cpp`).

107. M9, lane A (2026-11-10), the clock seam of `CounterPollThread`. The wait between two polls is the `PollClock` port (`poll_clock.hpp`): `SystemPollClock` sleeps the 500 ms period and is the default, `InMemoryPollClock` ends a wait when the test calls `tick()` and lets it count waits with `wait_until_waiting`, so the two thread tests no longer sleep or read a deadline. The destructor now calls `interrupt()` after setting its stop flag, so a wait ends at once. This changes one timing of the default: `SystemPollClock` skips its sleep only when the interrupt arrives before the wait, so a destructor that comes during a sleep still waits out the rest of the 500 ms, as before. The d92bd72c seams were checked against the records: `test_node_counters.cpp` and `test_core_counter_report.cpp` already pass their counter sets in; the sink side of `test_core_counter_report.cpp` and `test_parameter_report.cpp` stays on `LogCapture` after decisions 102 and 303, so they are `Small_` and unchanged.

108. M10, lane A (2026-11-10), the seams of `foundation/device`. `BlockMonitor` reads a `BlockClock` port (`SteadyBlockClock` by default, `InMemoryBlockClock` in tests). The block answer of `Sdl2AudioDevice::sdl_callback` (silence without a callback, the oversize check, the mono shortcut, the interleave) moved unchanged into `BlockServer`, which `Sdl2AudioDevice` calls from SDL2's thread; `InMemoryBlockDevice` is the `AudioDevice` that calls it when a test asks, with no driver, and it replaces the `CountingDevice` stand-in of `test_device_counter_report.cpp` (that test now raises `no_callback` by serving, not `overrun`). The four `Medium_Sdl2AudioDevice` tests became `Small_BlockServer` and `InMemoryBlockDevice` tests; the timeline test stays `Medium_` (it opens the process-wide `ProfileSession`). One real-driver test stays, `Medium_Sdl2AudioDevice.ABlockFromTheDriverReachesTheCallbackWithTheBlockSize`, waiting on a condition variable and not on a sleep, so the SDL thread-to-server call keeps a test. The oversize branch had no test before; it has two now. `test_device_log_category.cpp` became `compile_device_log_category.cpp`.

109. M8, lane A (2026-11-10), the bands of `test_dk_netlist.cpp`. The bias point is read after 0.2 s at 48 kHz (a settled bias is a DC solution, so the rate does not matter, and 9600 solves keep the test small). Only the coupling capacitor still moves: its current is at most Vp / (1 MOhm + 100 kOhm) times exp(-0.2 s / (1.1 MOhm * 22 nF)), about 64 nA, and it is the only imbalance at the plate. The cathode band is that current times 2.7 kOhm (0.17 mV) plus half a unit of the README's last printed digit (5e-5 V) plus the solver stop, 1e-9 of (1 + 273 V). The plate band is that current times 102.7 kOhm (6.6 mV) plus 5e-4 V plus the cathode band. The grid check compares the solver's grid-to-cathode voltage with minus the cathode voltage minus the grid current across the 68 kOhm stopper, to the cathode band without the print term. The convergence count is `Large_` (about one million solves at 384 kHz), and its 0.01 % is the spec ceiling. The two LTspice comparisons are `Medium_` fixtures over the three settings (a parameterised suite), with the missing-reference skip in `SetUp`; the -60 dB null and the 0.5 dB band are the spec values of D6b.4, named constants and not derived, because LTspice's own accuracy is not ours to bound. The new sizes were not run: all of these bands are derived by hand and unmeasured.

110. M13, lane A (2026-11-10), what is not done in `foundation/log`. `IsSushidspSetBySdLogLevel` is not rewritten: the record asks for the variable's effect through an injected environment reader, and the reader belongs to SushiLog, a package this repository does not own. The test still compares two names with literals. The other four records are applied: `Ceiling...` and `CategoryCarries...` are deleted, the evaluation test is split in two on a local counter, and the level tests use `LogLevelScope`.

300. M20, lane C (2026-11-10). The two `resize` records of `nodes/cabinet` that ask for a log port in the node (`AnUnpreparedNodeWritesOneWarnNamingTheCause`, `AnInstalledCabinetWritesNothing`) are not applied: the record leaves the constructor shape to the owner, and `LogCapture` belongs to M14. Until then those tests keep `LogCapture`, a scoped in-memory sink, and stay `Small_`. The splits of the status and the log line are applied, and the deleted subsets keep their coverage in the status tests. The identity-delay tolerance is `n * u * sum|x|` with n the FFT length of one partition, twice `latency_samples()`.

301. M21, lane C (2026-11-10). Three `resize` records of `nodes/capture` are not applied, because they need a port the owner has not shaped: `WriteAfterStopIsANoOp`, `DropsBlocksLargerThanTheRing` (its loop is rewritten, its size stays) and the logging test of `TrackRecorder`. They need a writer port beside `WavWriter` with an in-memory adapter and a log sink port, and `TrackRecorder` holds a concrete `WavWriter` and a thread. They stay `Medium_`, with their folders owned by a `TempFolder` guard. The sleep in `WritePerformsNoHeapAllocation` is removed with no seam: `AllocationTrap` counts the arming thread only (`thread_local` in `allocation_trap.cpp`), so the writer thread never needed a wait. Two thresholds have no derivation and are characterized, each to get a backlog entry from the orchestrator: the 0.01 amplitude band of `TrackPlayerAtHalfSpeedKeepsTheToneAmplitude` and the 80 dB image rejection of `VariRateReaderAtHalfSpeedKeepsTheSourceToneImageDown`; each is a named constant in its test file. `MetronomeStartsAClickOnEveryBeatFrame` runs ten seconds as a `Small_` test, and the five-minute run (14.4 million frames, over the one second limit of a small test) is `Large_MetronomeStartsAClickOnEveryBeatFrameOverFiveMinutes`. Risks for the final build, since no test ran: the speed rows of `VariRateReader` and the vari-rate peak bin are now exact comparisons, the pan-law bound is 8 float roundings, and the `TrackPlayer` bit-for-bit rows assume a pan of -1 gives a left gain of exactly 1.

302. M21, lane C (2026-11-10), the writer port ruled by the owner. `AudioFileWriter` (`append`, `close`, `frames_written`) is the port, in `modules/foundation/io/include/SushiDSP/foundation/io/audio_file_writer.hpp` beside `WavWriter`, which implements it. `WavWriter` opens in its constructor, so the port also carries `AudioFileWriterOpener`, a `std::function` from path, sample rate and channels to an open writer; `open_float32_wav_writer` is the production opener. `InMemoryAudioFileStore` (`in_memory_audio_file_writer.hpp`) is the in-memory adapter: it opens a recording per path, keeps the samples per channel, and refuses the paths it is told to. `TrackRecorder` takes the opener as a fourth constructor argument that defaults to `open_float32_wav_writer`, so `RecordingBus` and the host are unchanged. `WriteAfterStopIsANoOp`, the two block-larger-than-the-ring tests (now `Small_TrackRecorder` in `test_in_memory_writer.cpp`) and a new test that the recorder drains every accepted frame in order run on the store. The tests that read real files back (the ramp, the bus) stay `Medium_`. The store needs no lock: the writer thread is the only appender and `stop()` joins it before a test reads.

303. M21, lane C (2026-11-10), the logging seam ruled by the owner. `LogCapture` is the in-memory sink, and a test that reads only it and touches no file or device is `Small_`. `TrackRecorderLogsOneErrorNamingThePathWhenTheTakeFileCannotBeOpened` is now `Small_TrackRecorder.LogsOneErrorNamingThePathWhenTheTakeFileCannotBeOpened` on a store that refuses the path. The two cabinet records (`AnUnpreparedNodeWritesOneWarnNamingTheCause`, `AnInstalledCabinetWritesNothing`) were already `Small_` on `LogCapture` after decision 300, so no change was needed.

304. M22, lane C (2026-11-10). The filter tests of `nodes/dd3` measure a response by a quadrature estimate over a whole number of cycles, which is exact for a steady sine, and compare it with the exact digital response: each section is the bilinear low-pass 1/(1 - x^2 + jx/Q) with x = tan(pi f/fs)/tan(pi fc/fs), and each shelf is g(1 + jt/tz)/(1 + jt/tp). The tolerance is the float rounding bound of the chain, 2 (m + 1) u times the L1 norm of its impulse response, derived in `tests/nodes/dd3/README.md`. Values that could not be derived and stay named, characterized constants: `K_PROTOTYPE_AGREEMENT` (0.5 %, the digital response against the analogue prototype; an offline evaluation reads 0.446 % at worst, so the earlier comment of 0.4 % was right and the assertion of 1.5 % was loose). The pre-emphasis warp bound is derived (exp(m^2/2) - 1). Coverage changes: the pasted prototype values (1.009006, 0.871989, 1.003462, 1.010033, 0.7025132, 1.3184111, 3.5023974, 4.0021277) are replaced by the derivation they came from; the stop-band thresholds (0.15, 1e-4, 0.23, 2e-3) are replaced by the digital response at each probe; the sixth-octave sweep rounds each probe to the 20 Hz grid; the window test is split in two (anti-alias alone, delay path); the damping oracle is built from the capacitors, not from pasted 10-digit values.

305. M22, lane C (2026-11-10), the clock, converter, wet FIFO and compander files of `nodes/dd3`. Derived: the clock endpoints, the two geometric means, the delay times against the published ranges (0.703 % below at the fast end, 2.4 % above at the slow end, from N/N published and the 80 kHz the published times assume), the slew to a closed form with the float-log bound, the converter's saturation rows (exact), the SNR (6 \* 2048^2 with a six-sigma statistical bound of 0.1 dB), the compander's two laws (envelope ripple (2 ln 2 - 1) fc/f, twice, plus two float roundings), its round trip, its envelope crossing (the integer 327, from the closed form of the sub-stepped one-pole sum) and its time constant. Named characterized constants with no derivation: `K_RIPPLE_REACHES_DB` (-40 dB, the 50 Hz third harmonic; the first-order estimate is -22.4 dB) and `K_FOLD_BOUND_DB` (-70 dB, the carrier sideband beyond the main lobe), both in `test_ne570_compander.cpp`; `K_PROTOTYPE_AGREEMENT` of decision 304. Coverage changes: the clock acceptance row of 2 % at the endpoints and the 12 % delay-time tolerance are gone, implied by the exact endpoints and the derived deviations; the converter SNR test renders 48000 samples, not 480000, since 997 Hz at 48 kHz repeats every 48000; `Dd3ClockAdvancesOneQuarterOfTheRemainingDistancePerSubBlock` is renamed `Dd3ClockClosesTheDerivedFractionOfTheRemainingDistancePerSubBlock` (the name said a quarter, the figure is 0.2446570); the saturation test drops the check of the constant 2.4987793 V; `test_wet_fifo.cpp` gains the overflow test and the prime-on-real-samples test that `test_behaviour.cpp` held under `WetFifoPrimesThenDropsTheOldestWhenFull`, which the rewrite of `test_behaviour.cpp` must delete. Risks for the final build, since no test ran: the quadrature estimate bound of the filter tests (2 (m + 1) u times the L1 norm) is a model, not a proof, and the compander's twice-the-ripple bound is a model of a closed loop.

306. M22, lane C (2026-11-10), the `test_dd3_delay` files of `nodes/dd3`. Acceptance: the clock-curve test is deleted (the endpoints are in `test_dd3_clock.cpp`, the wet delay in `test_behaviour.cpp`); the echo, pitch-slur, battery and alias-floor tests are `Medium_` (they render 0.6 s, 1 s, 5 s and twenty one-second tones), the zero-latency test is rewritten to an impulse and stays `Small_` (760 samples), and the battery is a row table of fifteen runs, one medium test each. Derived: the pitch-slur bounds (the floor 23/25 and the ceiling from the per-period growth 4.125^(100/16800) of the log-linear clock, with one sample of quantisation each way, and the distance travelled with the R56/C52 lag and 1.5 periods of trace), the dry path at frame 0 (exact, 2 V in and out) and the silence up to the ring's length (causal stages), the wet delays (formula, with the float that holds log(fs)). Block-size invariance and the reset test compare bit for bit, since every stage runs one sample at a time. Behaviour: `Dd3DelayDeclaresItselfCorrectly` and `WetFifoPrimesThenDropsTheOldestWhenFull` deleted (the latter lives in `test_wet_fifo.cpp`); the parameter test is split in three, with the descriptor checks removed; the self-oscillation test is split into its two positions; the reset test now compares with a fresh node instead of asserting a 1e-6 silence; the 1 s and 2 s renders that only need a few repeats are cut to 1200, 2600, 4800 and 12000 samples. Named characterized constants with no derivation: `K_ECHO_PEAK_SLACK_SAMPLES` (48 in the acceptance file, 32 in the behaviour file), `K_ALIAS_FLOOR_BOUND_DB` (-40 dB; the measured floor is -55.32 dB and the -60 dB revisit threshold of Ruling 7 is missed, so the old printf and the old assertion disagreed and the assertion kept its value), `K_DIVERGENCE_TRIPWIRE` (32), `K_CENTROID_FALL_RATIO` (0.95), `K_DEAD_TAIL_RMS` (1e-4), `K_SUSTAINED_TAIL_RMS` (5e-3), `K_HELD_ENERGY_FLOOR` (1e-4), `K_CHECK_POINT_FIVE_BAND` (5 %) and `K_GOLDEN_RESIDUAL_BOUND_DB` (-80 dB). Output: the two golden tests are one row table; `render_dd3` uses `measure::render`. RT safety: the builder and the finiteness loop are helpers. The `SUSHIDSP_REGRESSION_TEST` macro is gone from this folder: the cases are acceptance tests, not defect regressions. Risks for the final build, since no test ran: the dry sample at frame 0 is exactly 1.0f and the stretch before the ring's length is exactly zero (assumes the compander and the quantiser return exact zero for zero input); the pitch-slur travel bound assumes the pitch follows the clock one to one; the battery rows are 240000 samples each and may need `Large_` if a row exceeds 10 s.

200. M15, lane B (2026-11-10). `test_biquad.cpp` measures every gain by projecting the output onto sine and cosine over whole cycles (bin k of an N-sample window), not by the sampled peak, which misses the crest by up to 0.9 %. The oracles are the analog prototypes of the RBJ designs evaluated at `omega = tan(pi f/fs)/tan(pi f0/fs)`, which the bilinear map reproduces exactly; the first-order shelf is `g (1 + jT/t_z)/(1 + jT/t_p)`, the one-pole low-pass `1/(1 + jT/t_c)`. The settle length is `2 ln(u)/ln(r)` with r the largest pole radius mapped through `z = (1 + t p)/(1 - t p)`, and the error bound is `4 u ||h||_1` (float rounding of input and output through the projection) measured from the filter's own impulse response. The impulse-invariant first-order high-pass is held to the analog RC with a budget of `omega_c^2` of relative gain; by hand it reads 2e-5 at fs = 48 kHz, fc = 100 Hz, against a budget of 1.7e-4. Derivations at risk, none run: the `omega_c^2` budget, the prototypes of the shelves and the peaking filter, and the `||h||_1` bound being measured from the filter under test. `BiquadSetCoefficientsLoadsThemVerbatim` is untouched.

201. M15, lane B (2026-11-10). `tests/foundation/math/fft_error_bound.hpp` holds the bounds the FFT and convolution tests share: `fft_relative_bound` is the radix-2 result of Higham (theorem 24.2) with twiddles rounded to float, and `partitioned_block_error_bound` is `(3 r + (P + 2) u) P ||x_block|| ||h_slice||_1` per output block, derived in `tests/foundation/math/README.md`. Both are worst-case bounds and the tests apply the default threshold of 16 on top of them. Derivation at risk: the convolution bound takes the product error against the larger spectrum (`||H||_inf <= ||h_slice||_1`), which is looser than the measured error but is not a proof of the constant 3.

The output length is now asserted exactly (48000), because the header says the count is rounded to the nearest sample and 44100 to 48000 is an exact ratio.

219. M30, lane B (2026-11-10), the derived bands. Pan gains hold to `K_GAIN_BOUND` = 5 u absolute: the angle `(pan + 1) pi_f / 4` has the float pi (0.47 u relative), one rounding in the sum and one in the product, at most `pi/2 * 2.5 u` = 3.9 u, and the library rounds the cosine or sine by u; the squared-gain sum holds to 17 u (`2 * sqrt(2) * 5 u + 2 u`). The hard-right left gain is `cos(pi_f / 2)` = -4.4e-8, which the bound passes and the old -80 dB limit also passed; the -80 dB limits are gone. The centre gain is compared with 1/sqrt(2), not -3.01 dB. The channel strip holds each sample to 11 u times its level (5 u for the angle, about 3.3 u for the float `pow` of the level at -20 dB, 2 u for the two products). The stereo strip holds to 10 rounded operations (the sum, the difference, the width product, the mid-side add, the balance and the level, with the `pow` at about 3 u). The bus tests now open the gates with a first block and then toggle, so a mute or solo really closes a gate; the oracle is the closed form `exp(-2560 / 240)` = 2.3e-5 of the muted input, held to 5 u of the unmuted sum. Before this change those tests muted before the first block, where the gate starts shut and the 10-block settle did nothing. The strip-through-bus test holds to 6 u (5 u for the angle, 1 u for the sum).

220. M30, lane B (2026-11-10), derivations at risk, all unrun. The `pow` cost of 3.3 u in the channel strip and the stereo strip is a hand estimate of 1 u for the library plus the exponent's division by 20 times `ln 10` times the exponent; it holds at -20 dB and does not hold at -60 dB. The settle residual assumes the gate recursion's float error stays under 0.06 u of the unmuted sum over 2560 frames (N u of a 2.3e-5 residual). The pan helper settles by comparing two successive blocks for equality, as the old one did; if a smoother never reaches equality the loop stops at 10000 blocks and the band then fails, not hangs.

Standing waves: the 262144-frame renders (rings, widths, corner) are `Large_` with their bounds unchanged, the corner and the cabinet and listener fades beside them; `Medium_RoomNodeFirstMode` (65536 frames, the first peak to half a bin, the mode's decay of 1.48 s leaves 10^-5.6 of its energy past the render), `Medium_RoomNodeCornerBass` (65536 frames, 12 dB, the slowest mode's 2.7 s leaves 10^-3.1) and two `Medium_RoomNodeWaveFade` cases at block 64 keep the direction (no step for a moved cabinet, the rebuilt bank under -6 dB: the steady render has reached 0.67 of its level at 64 blocks with a time constant of at most 0.39 s, and the restarted bank is behind it for every swap time). Seam (60 s, 18 renders of 65536 frames): `Large_RoomNodeSeam` is three rooms and six poses at 32768 frames (the slowest decay of 1.9 s leaves 0.7 % past it, 0.03 dB) as rows that share each room's octaves; `Medium_RoomNodeSeam` is the garage over two poses at 16384 frames (8 %, 0.36 dB). Hears-less-room: 128 blocks, not 188 (0.7 % of the reverberant energy past it). Direct-path windows: six blocks, which hold the 1500-frame window; the Woodworth renders four blocks (900-frame window); the tail render ends with its window at 101 blocks; the wave and listener-moved renders end past the fade (208 blocks, were 240); the 100-publish test is eight publishes, three generations of the swap slot walked more than twice. Everything that builds a set is `Medium_`; the records say `Small_` for the set tests, but a build is 0.55 s, so only the port, refusal-before-prepare, crossover-before-prepare, latency-before-prepare and microphone-model tests are `Small_`.

(2) `EXPECT_EQ` on copied values that cross JSON as doubles (0.5, -0.75, 0.25, 137.5, 0.77f, 0.42f) assumes nlohmann writes a shortest round-trip form; all but 0.77f and 0.9f are dyadic. (4) `Small_` is held to the 100 ms limit by the cost of building the tube screamer, the JCM 800 and the splitter and mixer a few times per case; no run has timed them. (5) The speed clamp at 0.5 is the old test's check, now in `SavesThePlaybackSpeed`.

(4) The export length 4800 + 376 * 64 frames assumes `place_clip` gives a clip of exactly 4800 frames and that an empty rig adds no tail. (5) The exported level bound is three roundings of 0.5 * 10^(-6.0206 / 20). (6) The migrated microphone level bound is |3.0103 - 10 log10(2)| + u * 3.0103, derived in `tests/applications/host_gui/README.md`. (7) The strip, balance and preset round trip compare floats exactly, which holds when nlohmann writes the double of a float in a form that parses back to it. 1100. Run fixes, lane fixn (2026-11-10), `MusicalClockDoesNotAccumulateErrorAcrossFiveMinutes`. The rewrite compared each beat start with `beat * 28800000 / 1374` by equality; the run gave 4799999 for 4800000 at beat 229 and 9599999 at beat 458. The clock floors `beat * step_q32 / 2^32`, and the step is the exact beat length rounded to the nearest 2^-32 frame, so a start is off by at most `beat * 2^-33`, under 1e-7 frames for 687 beats. The exact start is a multiple of 1/687 frames, so every beat that is not a whole frame is at least 1/687 frames from one and floors to the same frame; a beat whose exact start is a whole frame (`beat * 28800000 % 1374 == 0`, every 229th beat) can land just under it and read one frame early. The test now expects the exact frame, or one less for those beats. The code is right; the old test compared the clock with its own step and so could not see this.

1101. Run fixes, lane fixn, `TransportSeekClearsTheSpeedFraction`. `Transport::set_speed` clamps to [0.5, 2.0], so 0.3 played at 0.5; 64 frames then moved exactly 32 and left no remainder, and 3 frames after the seek moved 1.5 frames, so the position read 1001. The old test carried the same 0.3 and asserted nothing after the seek (`advance(0)`), so it passed without exercising the remainder. The case now uses 0.6: 64 frames move 38.4 (remainder 0.4), and after the seek one frame at 0.6 moves nothing, where a remainder left from before would reach 1.0 and move one. The rounded q32 speed is 0.4 frames of 2^-32 above 0.6, so the carried sum is 4294967322 against 2^32 = 4294967296, a margin of 26 units: it moves one frame, deterministically. The code is right.

1102. Run fixes, lane fixn, `EarlyDelayCeilingIsTheLongestCrossoverTimeInFrames/At96kHz`. The row held 16793 from 5760000 / 343 written as 16792.96. The quotient is 16793.0029, which rounds up to 16794; `max_early_delay_frames` is right. The 48 kHz row (2880000 / 343 = 8396.50, so 8397) was right. The row is 16794 and the comment carries both quotients.

1103. Run fixes, lane fixn, `BandPowerIsTheWorkedOutFigure/Hz125`. The hand figure was 5.39; 16 pi * 1.342 / (0.161 * 60) * 10^(-1.12 / 10) is 5.3957 from the rounded inputs, which written to two decimals is 5.40, not 5.39. The field gives 5.3975, which is 0.0025 from 5.40 against the half unit 0.005. The row is 5.40 and `tests/nodes/room/README.md` reads 5.40. The other five rows pass; their figures from the same inputs are 3.484, 1.052, 0.3754, 0.1538 and 0.06097. The old test held 5.39 to 0.5 percent, which hid the slip.

1104. Run fixes, lane fixn, `AppendWithUnknownDescriptorIdKeepsTheSlotAndBuildsNoNodeForIt`. Decision 706 called the old no-op assertion stale and said `SlotGraph::append` keeps the slot. It does not: `SlotGraph::publish` erases every slot whose node the catalogue did not build, as `applications/host_gui/README.md` ("`publish()` drops a slot whose node type the registry does not know") says, so the order held only the first slot and `descriptor_id` of the second read empty. The old test was right and decision 706's reading is withdrawn. The case is renamed `AppendWithUnknownDescriptorIdDropsTheSlotAndBuildsNoNodeForIt` and asserts the order `{a}`, an empty descriptor id for the dropped slot and one node; `tests/applications/host_gui/README.md` is updated. Whether a preset should be able to carry a slot the catalogue lacks is a design question for the owner; the code does not do it.

1105. Run fixes, lane fixn, `Rows/Medium_RigControllerCabinetRefusal`. `TestSupport::temp_fixture_path` prefixed the file with `current_test_info()->name()`, which for a parameterised case is `Test/Row`, so the path named a folder that does not exist. It now reads each '/' as '_'. The one helper is the only user of the name: Grep over `tests/` for `current_test_info`, `temp_directory_path` and `TempDir` finds no other file name built from a case name except `test_benchmark_harness.cpp`, whose cases are not parameterised.

1106. Run fixes, lane fixn, derivations at risk, all unrun. (1) The clock bound relies on the step error being at most half a unit of 2^-32 and on the beat start being the floor of `beat * step`; a different rounding of the step would move which beats read one early, though the allowance is by exact-frame beats only. (2) The 26-unit margin of decision 1101 rests on `llround` of `0.6 * 2^32`. (3) Decision 1104 asserts that the second slot's id is unused after the drop; the test reads `descriptor_id(unknown)` as empty.
1000. M1, lane fixc (2026-11-10), `test_silicon_diode.cpp` far-reverse conductance and reverse current, first run. The rewritten derivation had the tail slope wrong by a factor of 1000: past the lower knee the conductance is Is / (n Vt) e^-40 = 2.36e-25 S, not 2.3e-28 S. `ConductanceVanishes` (3 rows) asserted `< 1e-27`; the model returned 2.3626e-25, which is the correct value. It now compares to the closed form `kReverseConductance` within 6 operations of relative error (one division, one exponential and two products in the model, a division and a product in the test). `CurrentTakesItsRowValue` at -1000 V read -Is with 3 roundings of Is; the tangent departs from -Is by Is e^-40 plus the conductance times the distance past the knee (2.36e-22 A at 1000 V), so the bound of the three rows past the knee is `reverse_saturation_bound(volts)`, that departure plus 3 u Is. The code is right. Syntax-checked only.

1001. M1, lane fixc (2026-11-10), `test_silicon_diode.cpp` `AntiderivativeHasNoStepAcrossIt/UpperKnee`. The mean-value bound used the width 2 delta, but at 1.8 V the stored points edge +- 1e-9 are off that width by up to 2.2e-7 of it (one ulp of the edge is 2.2e-16), and the slope headroom of the bound is only 2.2e-8 of the step (1.19 A V at the upper knee, where the current is 5.9e8 A); the run showed error and bound equal to six digits. The bound now takes the width as the difference of the stored points with the steepest current at them, and adds the rounding of the exponential side, (3 n + 4) u of each antiderivative at the exponent argument n = 40, for both sides. The step threshold stays 1. Derivation at risk: the (3 n + 4) u count is the one `antiderivative_difference_bound` already uses, not a fresh count of the model's operations.

1002. M1, lane fixc (2026-11-10), `test_plate_swing.cpp` `IsTheIntegralOfTheBound/Zero`. The rounding term was 8 u of the antiderivative value, which near zero is h^2 / 2 = 5e-9. The antiderivative is H^2 (z + log1p(exp(-2 z)) - ln 2), three terms near 0.7 that cancel to z^2 / 2, so each value carries an error of order H^2 u (z + 1), not of its size; the run measured 8.3e-9 against a bound of 2.6e-11. The bound now counts 4 (z + 1) u H^2 per antiderivative value (side headroom chosen as the code does), plus u |x| / h of the slope for the grid rounding of x +- h, plus the two roundings of the bound. At x = 0 this is 3.3e-8 against the 8.3e-9 seen. Derivation at risk: 4 (z + 1) u H^2 is an upper bound summed from the six operations, not tight.

1003. M1, lane fixc (2026-11-10), `test_biquad.cpp` `StepResponseDecaysAsTheRealPolePowerN`. The filter returns a float, and after two seconds at 384 kHz the response is e^-133 = 1.8e-58 at 10.58 Hz, which rounds to zero (smallest float 1.4e-45), and e^-90.9 = 3.5e-40 at 7.23 Hz, a denormal whose quantum is 4e-6 of the value; that row passed only because its 1.76e-6 error sat inside 16 times the 1.2e-7 bound. A relative bound of 2 u_f holds only while the output is a normal float. The run now stops at n = min(2 fs - 1, 80 / omega); e^-80 = 1.8e-35 is above the float minimum normal 1.2e-38. The 2 Hz and 5 Hz rows keep their two seconds. The bound is unchanged. Derivation at risk: the 3 n u_d part (n up to 4.6e5) is the original estimate for a recursion that multiplies by R each step; it was not re-derived.

1004. M1, lane fixc (2026-11-10), `test_dk_circuit/test_behaviour.cpp` `DiodeClipperReachesTheTrueRootOfTheDiodeEquation`. The derived bound covered only the exit on a small step. `DampedNewtonSolver` also returns as soon as the residual's largest entry is at most 1e-12 (`K_ABSOLUTE_TOLERANCE`), which leaves |residual| / slope up to 1e-12 / slope; the old test measured 9.7e-13 V for this and allowed 1e-11. Against a bound of order 1e-15 the worst ratio was 629.6. The bound now adds `K_SOLVER_RESIDUAL_TOLERANCE / slope`. Derivation at risk: it takes the solver's residual rows to equal the scalar equation in volts (the rows are v - p - F f, and the two mirrored branches make them agree); not run. If the worst ratio stays above 16, the error comes from a third exit and is a finding for the solver, not for the bound.

1005. M1, lane fixc (2026-11-10), `test_power_amp_feedback.cpp` `PowerAmpFeedbackMatchesTheLadderSolvedByHand` (9 rows at 80 Hz, 600 Hz and 3 kHz). The by-hand oracle was wrong, not the leg: it returned the grid leak to ground, but in the netlist (`power_amp_feedback.cpp`) the leak returns to the tail node, which sits between the 22 k tail feed and the inverter's 1068.5 ohms, so the tail's own voltage moves the grid by 0.007 to 0.022 dB (the old test allowed 0.05 dB). The oracle is now the continuous-time nodal solution of the four nodes (split, divider, grid, tail) by Gaussian elimination, `analytic_gain_db`, and floors the presence rheostat at `SushiDSP::dk::K_MINIMUM_POT_OHMS` as the DK block does at presence 0 (1 ohm in 1.5 k, 0.003 dB, more than the 1.4e-5 dB bound). The bound is unchanged, warp plus measurement. The 10 kHz rows passed before only because the warp bound there is large; they now rest on the same exact oracle. Derivation at risk: the bilinear warp argument holds for the whole network because every capacitor is trapezoidal; not run.

1006. M1, lane fixc (2026-11-10), `test_graphic_equalizer.cpp` `GraphicEqualizerPerBandQShapesOneBandAndLeavesItsNeighboursAlone` (3 rows). The measurement, not the equalizer: the rms window is 48000 samples, a whole number of cycles for the uniform bank's 80, 240, 750, 2200 and 6600 Hz probes but not for 87.61, 723.43 and 743.48 Hz. The mean square of a sine over a window is A^2 / 2 (1 - e) with |e| at most |sin(N w)| / (N |sin w|); the gain in decibels carries that for the input and for the output, 2 x 10 / ln 10 x -ln(1 - |e|). It is 0 for the whole-cycle probes and 1.6e-2 dB at most for 87.61 Hz; the observed errors were 2.0e-3, 4.3e-4 and 1.2e-4 dB, and the centre probe passed because the phase shift is 0 there and the leak cancels in the ratio. `window_leakage_db` is added to the bound of both row tables.

1007. M1, lane fixc (2026-11-10), `test_fmv_tone_stack.cpp` `FmvToneStackMatchesTheSpiceMidSweepWithinItsWarpBound` (3 rows). Cannot be derived, so a named characterised constant: `K_SPICE_SOLVER_DB` = 0.05 dB, the old tolerance. An independent nodal solve in double of `jcm_fmv.asc` (the stack's netlist, exact decimals) differs from `jcm_fmv_ac.csv` by up to 0.0142 dB (middle 0.1; 0.0046 at 0.5, 0.0027 at 0.9), in a pattern that repeats every third point, and its worst deviation matches the stack's (0.0142237 dB in the run) to 1e-6; so the sweep, not the stack, carries it. A shift of the frequency axis does not explain it (the implied shift reaches 2 percent). The test keeps the warp, measurement and print terms and adds this constant. Recommend tightening it to 0.015 dB or regenerating the sweep; both are the owner's call.

1008. M1, lane fixc (2026-11-10), what was not run. Nothing here was built or run; each file was syntax-checked with `clang -fsyntax-only` through `compile_commands.json` with the worktree path (return code 0). The cases most likely to fail again after the run are the ones named as derivations at risk in 1001 to 1005.
1009. M1, lane dkroot (2026-11-10), `test_dk_circuit/test_behaviour.cpp` `DiodeClipperReachesTheTrueRootOfTheDiodeEquation`. Decision 1004 had the wrong units (the run still read 65.7 against 16). The solver's residual r = v - p - F i is in volts, but the circuit's output is w = v - r, not the iterate v, so g(w) = (1 - g'(v)) r + g''/2 r^2 and the measured |g(w)| / g'(w) is about |r|, not |r| / slope. The residual exit therefore allows 1e-12 V (K_SOLVER_RESIDUAL_TOLERANCE, not divided by the slope); 65.7 times the old bound of about 1.8e-14 is 1.2e-12, which is that. The step exit after a full step allows g'(w) step^2 / (2 Vt) (the slope multiplies). The bound is now 1e-12 (1 + 1e-12 / (2 Vt)) + g'(w) step^2 / (2 Vt) + 16 u (|source| + |w|), and the test also asserts `non_converged_samples()` is zero, so the budget-exhausted exit (best iterate, stale currents) is reported, not bounded. Derivation at risk: a damped final step is not covered by the quadratic form; if the case fails with zero non-converged samples, that is a solver finding. Run 1's ratio of 629.6 is not fully explained by this derivation alone, since its bound was not re-derived. Derivation in tests/foundation/math/dk/test_dk_circuit/README.md.

The fixture now lists `TestSupport::InMemoryRoom`, an in-memory adapter of the `RoomHost` port (`tests/support/in_memory_room.hpp`), under the room's id, through the new base `RigProductsInMemoryRoom` in `tests/support/rig_products.hpp`. Recorded in `tests/nodes/tubescreamer/test_tube_screamer_circuits/README.md`.

1208. M1, lane slow (2026-11-10), the cases between 0.1 and 1 s. Their inputs were not read for a shorter form, so each moved from `Small_` to `Medium_` by its suite name: `VariRateReaderHoldsItsRatioAtEveryLegalSpeed` (6 rows, 0.5 to 1.0 s) to `Medium_VariRateReaderRatio`; the four `FrequencyResponse` cases (0.22 to 0.97 s) to `Medium_FrequencyResponse`; `ResamplerRoundTripNulls` (3 rows) to `Medium_ResamplerRoundTrip`; `ResamplerSizesTheHistoryForAnUpsampleOnlyInstance` and `ResamplerReclampsThePhaseWhenARateChangeWidensTheKernel` to `Medium_Resampler`; the three `SincResampler` cases to `Medium_SincResampler`; `BandResponseFitMovesCoefficientsContinuouslyWithTheTarget` to `Medium_BandResponseFit`; `FmvToneStackLoadingTiltIsTheDecksOwn` (2 rows) to `Medium_FmvToneStackLoadingDeck`, which also moves the fast second case of that fixture; `UnoversampledTanhIsPoor` to `Medium_AliasFloor`; four `TubeScreamer` behaviour cases to `Medium_TubeScreamer`; three clip-block cases to `Medium_TubeScreamerCircuits` and the two small-signal rows to `Medium_TubeScreamerCircuitsClipSmallSignal`. Expected new size: Medium, each under 1 s. Not done: no input of these cases was shrunk, because none was read for a derivation (the move is the ruling's fallback); `TubeScreamerOutputStaysFiniteForTheRow/DcAtHighDrive` (0.122 s) stays `Small_` and over its limit. Recorded in the READMEs of the folders above.

## Adds

Promises a header or a port states that no test checks: `unit: promise | oracle | size`.

```
sine tone node: consecutive process calls continue the phase | metamorphic (two blocks of N frames equal one block of 2N) | small
sine tone node: prepare at a new sample rate changes the phase step | specified value | small
config: is_multi_config is true for Visual Studio and Xcode generators and false for Ninja | specified value | small
config: resolved_ninja prefers the configured ninja_exe over PATH | specified value | small
project commands: check returns the highest exit code of the checkers that ran | specified value | small
project commands: bench returns 1 when the tree holds no benchmark program | contract | medium
project commands: docs returns the code of the Doxygen driver | contract | medium
project commands: host returns 130 after Ctrl+C | contract | medium
project commands: test returns the build's code and runs no tests when the build fails | contract | medium
project commands: configure passes the Ninja make program and the compiler pair when a compiler is found | contract | medium
aliases: the notice is silent when the environment switch is 1 | contract | small
aliases: the notice is silent when the stream is not a terminal | contract | small
AudioImportQueue: the destructor drops queued jobs and waits for the import in progress | contract | medium
AudioImportQueue: drain keeps the entries already held in the output vector | contract | small
AudioImportCache: import decodes an mp3 to a WAV at the target rate | host reference | medium
RigTopology: set_di and set_di_position record the choice and change no slot | contract | small
RigTopology: a ThreeWay chain routes each path through the splitter and the mixer | contract | small
TopologyApplier: switching to Parallel wires the two strips and the bus | contract | small
RigController: one publish builds the same graph as a batch of edits before it | metamorphic | small
timeline_scale: set_pixels_per_frame ignores a value that is not finite or not above zero | contract | small
clap_rig_plugin: a valid stereo block returns CLAP_PROCESS_CONTINUE and counts nothing | contract | small
clap_rig_plugin: on_main_thread writes one WARN when a counter moved | contract with log sink | small
solver_counters: a circuit with no solves prints a zero share | specified message content | small
build_jcm_v1a: the grid leak to ground is K_GRID_LEAK_OHMS | specified value | small
TriodeStage: take_newton_tally() adds the Newton solves run since the last call and clears them | a second call adds nothing, and the total equals the solves counted over a run | small
PentodeStage: take_newton_tally() adds the Newton solves run since the last call and clears them | a second call adds nothing, and the total equals the solves counted over a run | small
FmvToneStack: process_sample() performs no heap allocation, as its header states it is audio-thread safe | allocation counter, in the form of Small_Fft's trap test | small
GraphicEqualizer: process_sample() performs no heap allocation, as its header states it is audio-thread safe | allocation counter, in the form of Small_Fft's trap test | small
ResonanceControl: process_sample() performs no heap allocation, as its header states it is audio-thread safe | allocation counter, in the form of Small_Fft's trap test | small
SpscRing: push and pop with a count are all or none, and a refusal changes nothing | exact values in order | small
SpscRing: clear() empties the ring and keeps its capacity | pop after clear fails; capacity() unchanged | small
```
